### Task 17: SiteStore port + the wt-web mount contract

**Files:**
- Create: `packages/sites/src/store.ts`
- Test: `packages/sites/src/store.test.ts`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `resolveSlug(name: string, isTaken: IsSlugTaken, traceId: string, deps?: ResolveSlugDeps) => Promise<Result<string>>` and `type IsSlugTaken = (slug: string) => Promise<boolean>` (Task 5); `toRows(draft: SiteDraft, options: ToRowsOptions): SiteRows` with `interface SiteRows { site: SiteInsert; pages: SiteRowsPage[] }` and `interface ToRowsOptions { workspaceId: string; slug: string; createdBy: string }` (Task 6); `interface SiteDraft { name: string; goal: string | null; pages: DraftPage[] }` (Task 4); `interface NormalizedSection { section: SectionContent; sort: number; raw: Record<string, unknown> }` (Task 3); `interface SiteDeployState` (Task 10); `Result<T>`, `ok`, `err`, `appError` from `@sahoda/shared`.
- Produces:
  ```ts
  export interface SiteStore {
    isSlugTaken(slug: string): Promise<boolean>
    createSite(rows: SiteRows): Promise<{ siteId: string }>
    recordDeploy(siteId: string, state: SiteDeployState): Promise<void>
  }
  export const SITE_STORE_METHODS = ['isSlugTaken', 'createSite', 'recordDeploy'] as const
  ```
  Task 18 re-exports `type SiteStore` from `./store`. Nothing else in the package imports it — it is a mount-point contract, not an internal dependency.

**Why `SITE_STORE_METHODS` exists.** `SiteStore` is a type and `import type` is erased by esbuild, so without one runtime value in the module `vitest` would report green against a missing `store.ts` and the suite would be exercising only `resolveSlug` and `toRows`. Exporting the verb set makes the red step fail for the honest reason and lets the test assert the seam is exactly three verbs wide. It is deliberately **not** re-exported from `src/index.ts` — it is a test pin, not public surface.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing test**

```typescript
// packages/sites/src/store.test.ts
import { describe, it, expect } from 'vitest'
import type { Result } from '@sahoda/shared'
import { ok, err, appError } from '@sahoda/shared'
import { SITE_STORE_METHODS, type SiteStore } from './store'
import type { SiteRows } from './map/to-rows'
import { toRows } from './map/to-rows'
import { resolveSlug } from './slug'
import type { SiteDraft } from './normalize/draft'
import type { NormalizedSection } from './normalize/section-content'
import type { SiteDeployState } from './deploy/port'

const FIXED_NOW = new Date('2026-07-19T10:00:00.000Z')
const TRACE_ID = 'trace-store-15'
const WORKSPACE_ID = '11111111-1111-4111-8111-111111111111'
const OTHER_WORKSPACE_ID = '22222222-2222-4222-8222-222222222222'
const CREATED_BY = 'user_2abcDEF'

/** noUncheckedIndexedAccess is on: index once, loudly, instead of sprinkling `!`.
 *  The same helper Task 6's to-rows.test.ts defines — a bare `files[0].content` is a
 *  compile error under `tsc --noEmit`, and test files are typechecked. */
const at = <T>(list: readonly T[], index: number): T => {
  const value = list[index]
  if (value === undefined) {
    throw new Error(`expected an element at index ${index}, got a list of length ${list.length}`)
  }
  return value
}

const HERO_SECTION: NormalizedSection = {
  section: {
    kind: 'hero',
    content: {
      headline: 'Fresh chai, delivered',
      subhead: 'Two hours, city wide',
      ctaLabel: 'Order now',
      ctaHref: 'https://chai.example/order',
    },
  },
  sort: 0,
  raw: { headline: 'Fresh chai, delivered' },
}

const CONTACT_SECTION: NormalizedSection = {
  section: { kind: 'contact', content: { headline: 'Talk to us', submitLabel: 'Send' } },
  sort: 1,
  raw: { headline: 'Talk to us' },
}

const DRAFT: SiteDraft = {
  name: 'Chai Point',
  goal: 'leads',
  pages: [
    {
      path: '/',
      title: 'Chai Point',
      seoDescription: 'Fresh chai delivered across the city.',
      sort: 0,
      sections: [HERO_SECTION, CONTACT_SECTION],
    },
  ],
}

const PREVIEW_DEPLOY: SiteDeployState = {
  deployer: 'fixture',
  status: 'live',
  preview: true,
  url: 'file:///tmp/sites/chai-point/index.html',
  bundleId: 'b3f1a2c4',
  scriptName: null,
  deployedAt: FIXED_NOW.toISOString(),
  error: null,
  history: [],
}

type FailingMethod = 'isSlugTaken' | 'createSite' | 'recordDeploy'

interface FakeStoreOptions {
  taken?: string[]
  failOn?: FailingMethod
}

interface FakeSiteStore extends SiteStore {
  readonly sites: Array<{ siteId: string; rows: SiteRows }>
  readonly deploys: Array<{ siteId: string; state: SiteDeployState }>
  readonly calls: string[]
}

/**
 * The in-memory reference implementation of the port. Deterministic ids stand in for
 * the service-role client's `gen_random_uuid()` — the point is that the STORE mints
 * them, never the package.
 */
const createFakeSiteStore = (options: FakeStoreOptions = {}): FakeSiteStore => {
  const taken = new Set(options.taken ?? [])
  const sites: Array<{ siteId: string; rows: SiteRows }> = []
  const deploys: Array<{ siteId: string; state: SiteDeployState }> = []
  const calls: string[] = []
  let minted = 0

  return {
    sites,
    deploys,
    calls,
    async isSlugTaken(slug: string): Promise<boolean> {
      calls.push(`isSlugTaken:${slug}`)
      if (options.failOn === 'isSlugTaken') throw new Error('connection pool exhausted')
      return taken.has(slug)
    },
    async createSite(rows: SiteRows): Promise<{ siteId: string }> {
      calls.push(`createSite:${rows.site.slug}`)
      if (options.failOn === 'createSite') {
        throw new Error('duplicate key value violates unique constraint "sites_slug_key"')
      }
      minted += 1
      const siteId = `site-${String(minted).padStart(4, '0')}`
      sites.push({ siteId, rows })
      taken.add(rows.site.slug)
      return { siteId }
    },
    async recordDeploy(siteId: string, state: SiteDeployState): Promise<void> {
      calls.push(`recordDeploy:${siteId}`)
      if (options.failOn === 'recordDeploy') throw new Error('deploy write failed')
      deploys.push({ siteId, state })
    },
  }
}

interface PersistContext {
  workspaceId: string
  createdBy: string
  traceId: string
}

/**
 * The composed call site, written here rather than exported: wt-web owns this glue at
 * the mount point. The test pins the contract it must honour — implementations THROW,
 * the caller returns a typed Result and never lets a rejection escape.
 */
const persistSite = async (
  draft: SiteDraft,
  store: SiteStore,
  ctx: PersistContext,
): Promise<Result<{ siteId: string; slug: string }>> => {
  try {
    const slug = await resolveSlug(draft.name, (candidate) => store.isSlugTaken(candidate), ctx.traceId)
    if (!slug.ok) return slug
    const rows = toRows(draft, {
      workspaceId: ctx.workspaceId,
      slug: slug.data,
      createdBy: ctx.createdBy,
    })
    const created = await store.createSite(rows)
    return ok({ siteId: created.siteId, slug: slug.data })
  } catch (error) {
    return err(
      appError('PROVIDER_ERROR', 'Could not save the site. Try again.', ctx.traceId, {
        cause: error instanceof Error ? error.message : String(error),
      }),
    )
  }
}

const persistDeploy = async (
  siteId: string,
  state: SiteDeployState,
  store: SiteStore,
  traceId: string,
): Promise<Result<null>> => {
  try {
    await store.recordDeploy(siteId, state)
    return ok(null)
  } catch (error) {
    return err(
      appError('PROVIDER_ERROR', 'Could not record the deploy. Try again.', traceId, {
        cause: error instanceof Error ? error.message : String(error),
      }),
    )
  }
}

describe('SITE_STORE_METHODS — the runtime pin on the port surface', () => {
  it('names exactly the three verbs the port declares, so the seam cannot widen unnoticed', () => {
    expect([...SITE_STORE_METHODS]).toEqual(['isSlugTaken', 'createSite', 'recordDeploy'])
  })

  it('is implemented in full by a conforming store, which is what makes the red step honest', () => {
    const store = createFakeSiteStore()

    for (const method of SITE_STORE_METHODS) {
      expect(typeof store[method]).toBe('function')
    }
  })
})

describe('SiteStore port — composes with resolveSlug + toRows end to end', () => {
  it('persists a draft and returns the id the STORE minted, proving the package generates none', async () => {
    const store = createFakeSiteStore()

    const result = await persistSite(DRAFT, store, {
      workspaceId: WORKSPACE_ID,
      createdBy: CREATED_BY,
      traceId: TRACE_ID,
    })

    expect(result).toEqual({ ok: true, data: { siteId: 'site-0001', slug: 'chai-point' } })
    expect(store.sites).toHaveLength(1)
    const rows = at(store.sites, 0).rows
    expect(rows.site.name).toBe('Chai Point')
    expect(rows.site.slug).toBe('chai-point')
    expect(rows.site.created_by).toBe(CREATED_BY)
    expect(rows.pages).toHaveLength(1)
    expect(at(rows.pages, 0).sections).toHaveLength(2)
  })

  it('probes isSlugTaken before createSite, because the global unique index is checked client-side first', async () => {
    const store = createFakeSiteStore()

    await persistSite(DRAFT, store, {
      workspaceId: WORKSPACE_ID,
      createdBy: CREATED_BY,
      traceId: TRACE_ID,
    })

    expect(store.calls).toEqual(['isSlugTaken:chai-point', 'createSite:chai-point'])
  })

  it('stores under the collision-resolved slug, proving the port actually gates slug choice', async () => {
    const store = createFakeSiteStore({ taken: ['chai-point', 'chai-point-2'] })

    const result = await persistSite(DRAFT, store, {
      workspaceId: WORKSPACE_ID,
      createdBy: CREATED_BY,
      traceId: TRACE_ID,
    })

    expect(result).toEqual({ ok: true, data: { siteId: 'site-0001', slug: 'chai-point-3' } })
    expect(at(store.sites, 0).rows.site.slug).toBe('chai-point-3')
    expect(store.calls).toEqual([
      'isSlugTaken:chai-point',
      'isSlugTaken:chai-point-2',
      'isSlugTaken:chai-point-3',
      'createSite:chai-point-3',
    ])
  })

  it('binds the site row to the caller workspace and leaves child rows unbound for the store to fill', async () => {
    const store = createFakeSiteStore()

    await persistSite(DRAFT, store, {
      workspaceId: WORKSPACE_ID,
      createdBy: CREATED_BY,
      traceId: TRACE_ID,
    })

    const rows = at(store.sites, 0).rows
    const firstPage = at(rows.pages, 0)
    expect(rows.site.workspace_id).toBe(WORKSPACE_ID)
    expect(rows.site.workspace_id).not.toBe(OTHER_WORKSPACE_ID)
    expect(Object.keys(firstPage.page)).not.toContain('workspace_id')
    expect(Object.keys(firstPage.page)).not.toContain('site_id')
    expect(Object.keys(at(firstPage.sections, 0))).not.toContain('workspace_id')
    expect(Object.keys(at(firstPage.sections, 0))).not.toContain('page_id')
  })

  it('keeps section order as the mapper assigned it, since the DB has no tiebreaker on sort', async () => {
    const store = createFakeSiteStore()

    await persistSite(DRAFT, store, {
      workspaceId: WORKSPACE_ID,
      createdBy: CREATED_BY,
      traceId: TRACE_ID,
    })

    const sections = at(at(store.sites, 0).rows.pages, 0).sections
    expect(sections.map((s) => s.kind)).toEqual(['hero', 'contact'])
    expect(sections.map((s) => s.sort)).toEqual([0, 1])
  })

  it('records the deploy state verbatim so preview:true survives the port unaltered', async () => {
    const store = createFakeSiteStore()

    const result = await persistDeploy('site-0001', PREVIEW_DEPLOY, store, TRACE_ID)

    expect(result).toEqual({ ok: true, data: null })
    expect(store.deploys).toEqual([{ siteId: 'site-0001', state: PREVIEW_DEPLOY }])
    expect(at(store.deploys, 0).state.preview).toBe(true)
    expect(at(store.deploys, 0).state.url).toBe('file:///tmp/sites/chai-point/index.html')
  })
})

const THROWING_CASES = [
  {
    failOn: 'isSlugTaken' as const,
    why: 'the collision probe is the first DB touch and a dead pool must not reject through the caller',
    expectedCause: 'connection pool exhausted',
  },
  {
    failOn: 'createSite' as const,
    why: 'a 23505 on the global slug unique index is the likeliest real-world failure',
    expectedCause: 'duplicate key value violates unique constraint "sites_slug_key"',
  },
]

describe('SiteStore port — a throwing implementation surfaces as a typed Result', () => {
  for (const testCase of THROWING_CASES) {
    it(`maps a throw in ${testCase.failOn} to PROVIDER_ERROR — ${testCase.why}`, async () => {
      const store = createFakeSiteStore({ failOn: testCase.failOn })

      const result = await persistSite(DRAFT, store, {
        workspaceId: WORKSPACE_ID,
        createdBy: CREATED_BY,
        traceId: TRACE_ID,
      })

      expect(result.ok).toBe(false)
      if (result.ok) throw new Error('unreachable: expected a failed Result')
      expect(result.error.code).toBe('PROVIDER_ERROR')
      expect(result.error.traceId).toBe(TRACE_ID)
      expect(result.error.message).toBe('Could not save the site. Try again.')
      expect(result.error.details).toEqual({ cause: testCase.expectedCause })
    })
  }

  it('resolves rather than rejects when the store throws, so no unhandled rejection escapes the mount point', async () => {
    const store = createFakeSiteStore({ failOn: 'createSite' })

    await expect(
      persistSite(DRAFT, store, {
        workspaceId: WORKSPACE_ID,
        createdBy: CREATED_BY,
        traceId: TRACE_ID,
      }),
    ).resolves.toMatchObject({ ok: false })
  })

  it('maps a throw in recordDeploy to PROVIDER_ERROR and records nothing, so no half-written deploy is claimed', async () => {
    const store = createFakeSiteStore({ failOn: 'recordDeploy' })

    const result = await persistDeploy('site-0001', PREVIEW_DEPLOY, store, TRACE_ID)

    expect(result.ok).toBe(false)
    if (result.ok) throw new Error('unreachable: expected a failed Result')
    expect(result.error.code).toBe('PROVIDER_ERROR')
    expect(result.error.details).toEqual({ cause: 'deploy write failed' })
    expect(store.deploys).toEqual([])
  })

  it('never leaks a store failure as a success with a plausible id, which would strand an unsaved site', async () => {
    const store = createFakeSiteStore({ failOn: 'createSite' })

    const result = await persistSite(DRAFT, store, {
      workspaceId: WORKSPACE_ID,
      createdBy: CREATED_BY,
      traceId: TRACE_ID,
    })

    expect(result.ok).toBe(false)
    expect(store.sites).toEqual([])
  })
})
```

Expected: FAIL at typecheck with `src/store.test.ts(6,45): error TS2307: Cannot find module './store' or its corresponding type declarations.`, then FAIL at vitest with `Error: Failed to load url ./store`. Check typecheck first: `SiteStore` itself is imported with `import type` and is erased at runtime, which is precisely why `store.ts` also exports the runtime constant `SITE_STORE_METHODS` — without one real value in the module, vitest alone would report green against a missing file.

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the implementation**

```typescript
// packages/sites/src/store.ts
import type { SiteRows } from './map/to-rows'
import type { SiteDeployState } from './deploy/port'

/**
 * The persistence port for the sites family — THE seam between @sahoda/sites and wt-web.
 * This package owns the interface; wt-web implements it with the service-role Supabase
 * client at the mount point. Nothing inside this package imports an implementation: every
 * function left of this port is pure, which is why the whole package tests without a DB.
 *
 * Implementations THROW on failure. They must NOT swallow errors and must NOT return a
 * plausible-looking id on a failed write — the caller catches and maps to a
 * PROVIDER_ERROR `Result`. A silent failure here strands a site the user believes exists.
 *
 * ── HARD REQUIREMENTS ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
 *
 * 1. THE PUBLIC LEAD-FORM ROUTE MUST DERIVE `workspace_id` FROM THE SITE ROW, VIA THE
 *    REQUEST HOST/SLUG — NEVER FROM THE FORM PAYLOAD.
 *    `leads.site_id` is the only non-composite FK in the sites family: every other child
 *    table is bound by a composite `(parent_id, workspace_id)` FK that makes a mismatched
 *    tenant physically impossible. `leads` has no such guard. A forged `workspace_id` in a
 *    submitted form therefore writes a lead into an arbitrary workspace, and the database
 *    cannot stop it. The route is the only boundary. Look the site up by host/slug, read
 *    `workspace_id` off that row, and ignore any tenant field the client sent.
 *
 * 2. `leads` HAS NO INSERT POLICY — DELIBERATELY. The public form is unauthenticated, so
 *    the route inserts with the service role, which bypasses RLS entirely. That makes the
 *    route the whole security perimeter: verify Turnstile AND apply the rate limit BEFORE
 *    the insert, never after. (The TURNSTILE_* env keys are not yet provisioned — see
 *    REQUESTS.md item 2; the route must fail closed until they exist, never skip the check.)
 *
 * 3. `createSite` MUST INSERT site → pages → sections IN ONE TRANSACTION, AND OWNS ALL ID
 *    GENERATION. `SiteRows` deliberately omits `site_id`/`page_id`/`workspace_id` from the
 *    child rows: the implementation mints each parent id, stamps it and the workspace onto
 *    the children, and rolls the whole thing back on any failure. A partial insert leaves a
 *    site with no pages — a site that renders empty and reads as a successful generation.
 *
 * 4. `recordDeploy` MUST FILTER BY WORKSPACE AS WELL AS BY ID. The signature takes only
 *    `siteId`, so the implementation has to re-read the tenant off the site row and add
 *    `.eq('workspace_id', …)`; `sites` carries `unique (id, workspace_id)` to make that
 *    filter cheap. A service-role update keyed on `id` alone is one missing predicate away
 *    from writing deploy state onto another tenant's site.
 */

/**
 * The port's verb set as a RUNTIME value. `SiteStore` is a type and `import type` is erased
 * by esbuild, so without this constant `store.test.ts` would report green against a missing
 * `store.ts` and would be exercising only `resolveSlug` and `toRows`. Exporting one real
 * value makes the red step fail for the honest reason, and lets the test assert the seam is
 * exactly three verbs wide. Deliberately NOT re-exported from `src/index.ts` — it is a test
 * pin, not part of the public surface.
 */
export const SITE_STORE_METHODS = ['isSlugTaken', 'createSite', 'recordDeploy'] as const

export interface SiteStore {
  /**
   * True when `slug` is already used by ANY workspace. `sites.slug` is globally unique,
   * not per-tenant, so this probe must query without a workspace filter — a tenant-scoped
   * query returns false for a slug another tenant holds and the insert then fails on 23505.
   */
  isSlugTaken(slug: string): Promise<boolean>

  /**
   * Insert the site and all of its pages and sections transactionally; returns the new
   * site id. See hard requirement 3. Throw on failure.
   */
  createSite(rows: SiteRows): Promise<{ siteId: string }>

  /**
   * Persist deploy state onto `sites.deploy` (and `sites.last_deployed_at` when
   * `state.deployedAt` is non-null). Write `state` verbatim: `preview: true` means the URL
   * is NOT public, and dropping or rewriting that flag turns an honest preview into a fake
   * live site. Throw on failure.
   */
  recordDeploy(siteId: string, state: SiteDeployState): Promise<void>
}
```

```typescript
// packages/sites/src/index.test.ts
import { describe, it, expect } from 'vitest'
import { readFileSync } from 'node:fs'
import { fileURLToPath } from 'node:url'
import * as sites from './index'

const INDEX_SOURCE = readFileSync(fileURLToPath(new URL('./index.ts', import.meta.url)), 'utf8')

/** Every value the package intends to expose. Types erase at runtime and are checked by tsc. */
const EXPECTED_VALUE_EXPORTS = [
  'SITES_PACKAGE',
  // render/escape — the security gate
  'escapeHtml',
  'escapeAttr',
  'safeUrl',
  'stripControl',
  // normalize
  'normalizeDraft',
  // slug
  'RESERVED_SLUGS',
  'slugify',
  'resolveSlug',
  // map
  'toRows',
  // theme
  'parseOklch',
  'formatOklch',
  'guardPrimaryForeground',
  'BRAND_VAR_NAMES',
  'brandSkinVars',
  'themeCss',
  // render
  'renderBundle',
  'LEAD_FORM_FIELDS',
  // deploy
  'SiteDeployStateSchema',
  'SiteDeployHistoryEntrySchema',
  'createFixtureDeployer',
  'createCloudflareDeployer',
  // transport
  'fetchTransport',
  'fixtureTransport',
  'routedTransport',
].sort()

/** Deliberately internal — each with the reason it must stay internal. */
const FORBIDDEN_EXPORTS = [
  { name: 'renderHero', why: 'section renderers are reachable only through renderBundle, which guarantees the escape pass' },
  { name: 'renderFeatures', why: 'section renderers are reachable only through renderBundle, which guarantees the escape pass' },
  { name: 'renderOffer', why: 'section renderers are reachable only through renderBundle, which guarantees the escape pass' },
  { name: 'renderTestimonials', why: 'section renderers are reachable only through renderBundle, which guarantees the escape pass' },
  { name: 'renderFaq', why: 'section renderers are reachable only through renderBundle, which guarantees the escape pass' },
  { name: 'renderContact', why: 'section renderers are reachable only through renderBundle, which guarantees the escape pass' },
  { name: 'renderDocument', why: 'a single page rendered outside the bundle would ship without the stylesheet' },
  { name: 'renderStylesheet', why: 'the stylesheet is bundle-internal and has no standalone consumer' },
  { name: 'renderLeadForm', why: 'form markup outside a rendered document would post to an unbound action' },
  { name: 'renderLink', why: 'the single link-emitting path is an internal invariant of the renderers, not a utility for callers' },
  { name: 'renderCta', why: 'a CTA rendered outside a section carries no surrounding markup and no section class' },
  { name: 'normalizeSection', why: 'a section normalized outside normalizeDraft skips path dedupe and page bounds' },
  { name: 'normalizePath', why: 'raw normalizers let a caller build a draft that bypasses the traversal guard' },
  { name: 'pathToFile', why: 'raw normalizers let a caller build a draft that bypasses the traversal guard' },
  { name: 'oklchToRgb', why: 'colour-space math is an implementation detail of the readability guard' },
  { name: 'rgbToOklch', why: 'colour-space math is an implementation detail of the readability guard' },
  { name: 'relativeLuminance', why: 'colour-space math is an implementation detail of the readability guard' },
  { name: 'contrastRatio', why: 'exposing it invites callers to reimplement the guard with a different threshold' },
]

describe('@sahoda/sites public surface', () => {
  it('exports exactly the documented value surface, so an accidental export is a failing test', () => {
    expect(Object.keys(sites).sort()).toEqual(EXPECTED_VALUE_EXPORTS)
  })

  it('carries the package sentinel so a mis-wired workspace alias is caught at the call site', () => {
    expect(sites.SITES_PACKAGE).toBe('@sahoda/sites')
  })

  it('uses explicit named re-exports only, because export * would leak internals silently', () => {
    expect(INDEX_SOURCE).not.toMatch(/export\s+\*/)
  })

  it('wires the real escape module rather than a stub, proving the barrel is not shadowing the gate', () => {
    expect(sites.escapeHtml('<script>alert(1)</script>')).toBe(
      '&lt;script&gt;alert(1)&lt;/script&gt;',
    )
    expect(sites.safeUrl('javascript:alert(1)')).toBeNull()
  })

  it('wires the real deploy schema, proving the barrel re-exports a usable zod schema', () => {
    const parsed = sites.SiteDeployStateSchema.safeParse({
      deployer: 'fixture',
      status: 'live',
      preview: true,
      url: 'file:///tmp/site/index.html',
      bundleId: 'b3f1a2c4',
      scriptName: null,
      deployedAt: '2026-07-19T10:00:00.000Z',
      error: null,
      history: [],
    })
    expect(parsed.success).toBe(true)
  })

  for (const forbidden of FORBIDDEN_EXPORTS) {
    it(`does not export ${forbidden.name} — ${forbidden.why}`, () => {
      expect(Object.keys(sites)).not.toContain(forbidden.name)
    })
  }
})
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the implementation**

```typescript
// packages/sites/src/index.ts
// @sahoda/sites — turns a SiteGenerateOutput into (a) rows for the frozen
// sites/site_pages/site_sections schema and (b) a static HTML/CSS bundle, then hands the
// bundle to a Deployer. This package never calls mesh, never touches a database, and never
// calls fetch: SiteStore and Deployer are the only async seams, and wt-web implements them.
//
// DELIBERATELY NOT EXPORTED:
//   src/render/sections/*        — the six per-kind renderers. renderBundle is the ONLY way
//                                  in, because it is what guarantees every interpolation has
//                                  passed through escape.ts. A directly callable renderer is
//                                  an escape hatch around the security gate.
//   renderDocument / renderStylesheet / renderLeadForm
//                                — bundle-internal. A page rendered alone ships without its
//                                  stylesheet; a form rendered alone posts to an unbound
//                                  action. Neither has an honest standalone use.
//   normalizeSection / normalizePath / pathToFile
//                                — the raw normalizers. Reachable only through
//                                  normalizeDraft, which is where path dedupe, the traversal
//                                  guard and the page bound are applied. Exposing them lets a
//                                  caller assemble a SiteDraft that skipped all three.
//   oklchToRgb / rgbToOklch / relativeLuminance / contrastRatio
//                                — colour-space math internal to the Readability Guard.
//                                  guardPrimaryForeground is the supported entry point; a
//                                  second caller would inevitably pick a different threshold.
//   renderLink / renderCta       — the package's single link-emitting path and its one
//                                  caller. Exporting them would hand a consumer a way to
//                                  emit an anchor outside a section, which is the exact
//                                  shape of the mistake renderLink exists to prevent.
//   appendHistory / DEPLOY_HISTORY_LIMIT
//                                — history capping is the deployers' job, and the schema's
//                                  .max(5) is what the persisted value is checked against.
//   SITE_STORE_METHODS           — a runtime pin so store.test.ts can fail for the right
//                                  reason; it is not a contract for callers.
export const SITES_PACKAGE = '@sahoda/sites' as const

// ── Escaping: the security gate. Every interpolation in this package goes through it, and
// the lead-form route should use it for anything it echoes back. ────────────────────────
export { escapeHtml, escapeAttr, safeUrl, stripControl } from './render/escape'

// ── Normalization: model output → a validated SiteDraft. ────────────────────────────────
export { normalizeDraft } from './normalize/draft'
export type { SiteDraft, DraftPage, NormalizeOptions } from './normalize/draft'
export type {
  NormalizedSection,
  SectionContent,
  HeroContent,
  FeaturesContent,
  FeatureItem,
  OfferContent,
  TestimonialsContent,
  TestimonialItem,
  FaqContent,
  FaqItem,
  ContactContent,
} from './normalize/section-content'

// ── Slugs: sites.slug is GLOBALLY unique, so resolution needs the store's predicate. ─────
export { RESERVED_SLUGS, slugify, resolveSlug } from './slug'
export type { IsSlugTaken, ResolveSlugDeps } from './slug'

// ── Row mapping: SiteDraft → insert rows. Child rows omit the ids the store mints. ───────
export { toRows } from './map/to-rows'
export type { SiteRows, SiteRowsPage, ToRowsOptions } from './map/to-rows'

// ── Theme: workspace tokens → the seven brand CSS vars, contrast-guarded. ────────────────
export { parseOklch, formatOklch } from './theme/oklch'
export type { Oklch, Rgb } from './theme/oklch'
export { guardPrimaryForeground } from './theme/readability'
export type { GuardedPair, ForegroundToken } from './theme/readability'
export { BRAND_VAR_NAMES, brandSkinVars, themeCss } from './theme/css'

// ── Rendering: the bundle entry point, plus the field names the lead route must accept. ──
export { renderBundle } from './render/bundle'
export { LEAD_FORM_FIELDS } from './render/form'
export type { RenderContext } from './render/context'

// ── Deploy: the port, its state schema, and the two deployers. ───────────────────────────
export { SiteDeployStateSchema, SiteDeployHistoryEntrySchema } from './deploy/port'
export type {
  Deployer,
  DeployContext,
  SiteBundle,
  BundleFile,
  SiteDeployState,
  SiteDeployHistoryEntry,
} from './deploy/port'
export { createFixtureDeployer } from './deploy/fixture'
export type { FixtureDeployerDeps, WriteBundleFile } from './deploy/fixture'
export { createCloudflareDeployer } from './deploy/cloudflare'
export type { CloudflareConfig, CloudflareDeployerDeps } from './deploy/cloudflare'

// ── Transport: production wires fetchTransport; tests replay recorded fixtures. ──────────
export { fetchTransport, fixtureTransport, routedTransport } from './transport'
export type { Transport, TransportRequest, TransportResponse } from './transport'

// ── The persistence port wt-web implements with the service-role client. ─────────────────
export type { SiteStore } from './store'
```

- [ ] **Step 5: Write the package CLAUDE.md**

Create `packages/sites/CLAUDE.md`:

```markdown
# packages/sites

Turns a `site_generate` mesh output into rows for the frozen `sites` / `site_pages` /
`site_sections` schema **and** a static HTML/CSS bundle, then hands the bundle to a `Deployer`.
Never calls mesh, never touches a database, never calls `fetch` — `SiteStore` (`src/store.ts`)
and `Deployer` (`src/deploy/port.ts`) are the only async seams, and wt-web implements both at
the mount point. Everything else is a pure function. Fixtures in `./fixtures`.

- **Every interpolation goes through `src/render/escape.ts`. No raw-HTML pass-through, ever.**
  Model copy ships to a customer's live domain, so an injected `<script>` executes in the
  tenant's origin on their visitors. Use `escapeHtml` for text, `escapeAttr` for attribute
  values, `safeUrl` for hrefs (`http`/`https`/`mailto`/`tel` only — a rejected URL DROPS the
  link, it never emits a dead one). No model string reaches `<style>`, `<script>`, or an
  event-handler attribute; the generated document contains no inline JavaScript at all.
  The escape suite is a package-level gate: a failure there blocks the package, not the file.
- **No fake links.** A deployer returns a genuinely reachable `url`, or `preview: true`, or a
  typed error — never a plausible-looking URL it cannot back. `preview` is a required field on
  `SiteDeployState` precisely so it cannot be forgotten, and the UI must surface it.
  `fixtureDeployer` always returns `preview: true` + a `file://` url. `cloudflareDeployer` with
  no config returns `PROVIDER_ERROR "cloudflare-not-configured"`. The base domain is injected
  config, never hardcoded, so a wrong domain cannot manufacture a believable URL from inside
  the renderer. (PRD:38, TSD §2 — "kills the fake-deploy problem".)
- **The model's output is advisory.** `site_sections.content` is `z.record(z.string(),
  z.unknown())`; there is no per-kind shape in the repo. Normalize tolerantly with the local
  per-kind schemas — every field optional, coerce where sane, a string `items` becomes a
  one-item list, unknown keys are dropped into `dropped: string[]`, not fatal. A bad generation
  must never become a hard failure OR an injection vector. Only genuinely empty output
  (`pages: []`) is a `VALIDATION_ERROR`.

Types/schemas import from `@sahoda/shared` only. Factories not classes; every nondeterministic
input injected and defaulted (`now?: () => Date`). Files < 300 lines, one section renderer per
file, colocated `*.test.ts`, no vitest config, no mocking library.
```

- [ ] **Step 6: Write REQUESTS.md**

Create `packages/sites/REQUESTS.md`:

```markdown
# packages/sites — cross-lane requests

Three items this package cannot legally fix from wt-pub. Each is filed rather than absorbed
because the file that needs the change is owned by another lane, and a stub written here would
be a fake — exactly what the Alpha Gate's "no fake states anywhere" rule prohibits.

## 1. No anon-client RLS tests for the entire sites family — **owner: wt-db**

**Evidence:** `packages/db/tests/rls.test.ts` contains no anon-client cross-tenant test for
`sites`, `site_pages`, `site_sections`, `leads`, or `workspace_themes`. All five tables exist
(`20260718000007_sites.sql`, `20260718000008_experience_ops.sql`) with policies applied.

**Why filed:** root `CLAUDE.md` requires "every table: `workspace_id` + RLS + anon-client test".
Five tables are short one. This trips the `/ship` gate for whoever ships next, not only for
Sites v0. Only wt-db edits `packages/db`, so wt-pub cannot add them.

**Asked for:** one anon-client cross-tenant read test per table, plus — for `leads`
specifically — an anon-client INSERT test asserting the insert is **rejected**, since `leads`
has no INSERT policy by design and the public form route depends on that being true.

## 2. No `TURNSTILE_*` env key exists — **owner: founder / wt-web**

**Evidence:** `docs/12_Build_Companion` §7 `.env.example` provisions no Turnstile key of any
name. Four places assert Turnstile anti-spam on the lead form, including the `leads` migration's
own comment and `LeadInsertSchema`'s doc comment in `packages/shared/src/db/sites.ts`
("validated + Turnstile-checked server-side before insert").

**Why filed:** `leads` has no INSERT policy, so the public route inserts with the service role
and bypasses RLS entirely — the route is the whole security perimeter. Without a site key and a
secret key it cannot spam-check at all. wt-pub owns neither `.env*` (explicitly off-limits) nor
the route. The route must fail closed until the keys exist; it must never skip the check.

**Asked for:** `NEXT_PUBLIC_TURNSTILE_SITE_KEY` and `TURNSTILE_SECRET_KEY` added to §7
`.env.example` and provisioned in the deployment environment.

## 3. "1 free site/quarter on paid plans" is unimplemented — **owner: wt-billing**

**Evidence:** FSD M6 specifies 100 credits plus "1 free site/quarter on paid plans".
PRD §7.1 states Free = "0 (preview only)". PRD §10 still lists this as an open question.
No quota, counter, or entitlement check for it exists anywhere in the repo.

**Why filed:** the three sources disagree, and one of them is still an open question — that is a
product decision, not an implementation gap. Sites v0 ships the debit path only
(`site_generate: 100` from `pricing.config.json`, run inside `withCredits` by the caller) and
**does not fake the quota**. A hand-rolled quarterly counter here would be a second, divergent
entitlement source next to `packages/billing`.

**Asked for:** the ruling, then the entitlement in `packages/billing` alongside the existing
plan gates. Until it lands, every site generation is a straight 100-credit debit.
```

---

## Appendix: Drafter Concerns and Rulings

Seven parallel drafters each filed a concerns block against the section they wrote. They are
collected here, grouped under the task they relate to in this plan's final numbering, with a
ruling on each. The concern text is the drafter's — unedited except for task numbers updated to
match the renumbering — and the **RULING** line is the decision.

#### Task 1 — `escape.ts`

1. **Style split with the lane it says to copy.** The contract specifies `export const fn = (…) => …` for every function, but `packages/publishing/src/transport.ts` — named as the style reference — uses `export function`. I followed the contract. Worth reconciling before Task 16 ports `transport.ts` into this package, or the same file will exist in two shapes in two packages.

   **RULING:** follow the contract (`export const`); the divergence from `packages/publishing`'s `export function` is cosmetic and not worth a cross-package refactor mid-sprint.

2. **`safeUrl` returns a cleaned string, and the type does not say so.** It strips control/whitespace characters and returns the *modified* value, which the caller must still run through `escapeAttr`. A `string | null` return makes it easy for a Task 12 renderer to interpolate it directly into `href="…"`. A branded type (`SafeUrl`) would make the missing escape a compile error rather than a review catch. Flagging only; I did not change the signature.

   **RULING:** resolved by `renderLink` (Task 12). It is now the only link-emitting path in the package, so no renderer assembles an `href` and the missing-`escapeAttr` mistake is unavailable rather than merely tested-against. A branded `SafeUrl` is no longer needed.

3. **`stripControl` is the one function typed `string` rather than `unknown`.** Everything else in `escape.ts` hardens against non-string input. If a caller ever reaches it with model output directly it will throw on `.replace`. Kept as specified.

   **RULING:** accepted; `stripControl` is internal and every caller reaches it through `escapeHtml`/`escapeAttr`, which harden first.

#### Task 2 — `path.ts`

1. **`normalizePath` is asymmetric on absence.** `''` → `'/'` but `undefined` → `null`. A model that omits `path` entirely on its only page gets the page dropped, while one that sends `""` gets the home page. Given `SiteGenerateOutputSchema` requires `path: z.string()`, the `undefined` branch is defense-in-depth only — but the asymmetry is surprising and Task 4 should be aware of it.

   **RULING:** accepted — `SiteGenerateOutputSchema` makes `path` required, so the `undefined` branch is defense-in-depth only; either way Task 4 records the drop in `dropped`, which is the honest outcome. Task 4's tests pin both branches.

2. **The segment allowlist goes beyond design §4.1**, which specifies only a denylist (`.`, `..`, separators, NUL, controls). My implementation additionally rejects non-ASCII and spaces, so `/café` or `/about us` drops the page rather than rendering it. I judged an allowlist correct at a filesystem boundary, and the alternative — slugifying here — would create a second, divergent slug implementation next to Task 5's. If the product wants those pages kept, the fix is to route `path` through Task 5's `slugify` per segment before this guard, which is a Task 4 change, not a change here.

   **RULING:** accepted — an allowlist is correct at a filesystem boundary, and slugifying here would create a second, divergent slug implementation next to Task 5's. If the product wants `/café` kept, route `path` through `slugify` per segment inside Task 4, not here.

3. **`MAX_PATH_LENGTH`/`MAX_SEGMENTS` are unexported**, per the contract listing only two exports from `path.ts`. This means Task 4 cannot report *why* a path was dropped with any more precision than "invalid path" in its `dropped` list.

   **RULING:** accepted — "invalid path" is a sufficient `dropped` label for v0, and exporting the limits would invite a caller to re-implement the check instead of calling it. Task 2 no longer re-exports anything from `path.ts` at all.

#### Task 3 — `section-content.ts`

1. **`NormalizedSection` nests awkwardly.** `normalizeSection` returns `{ section: NormalizedSection; dropped }` where `NormalizedSection` itself has a `section: SectionContent` field, so callers write `result.section.section.kind`. Followed as locked; a flatter `{ content: SectionContent; sort; raw; dropped }` would read better if the contract is ever reopened.

   **RULING:** accepted as locked for this sprint; flatten to `{ content; sort; raw; dropped }` post-Alpha if the contract reopens.

2. **`raw: Record<string, unknown>` on `NormalizedSection` is non-null even when the model sent a scalar.** I return `{}` in that case, which is honest but indistinguishable from "the model sent an empty object". Only matters for debugging.

   **RULING:** accepted — `{}` is the honest value for a non-object bag, and the distinction only matters while debugging a generation, where `dropped` already names what was discarded.

3. **Task 3 depends on `stripControl` from `render/escape.ts` (Task 1)**, which makes `normalize/` depend on `render/`. It is the right behavior (control characters must not reach stored content), but if the layering is meant to be strict, `stripControl` belongs in a shared `text.ts` that both import.

   **RULING:** accepted; control characters must not reach stored content, so the dependency is the right behaviour. Note it as a candidate `src/text.ts` extraction post-Alpha, so `normalize/` and `render/` would both import a leaf instead of one importing the other.

4. **`faq` items arriving as bare strings make the whole section unsalvageable**, because a question with no answer renders nothing. That is a deliberate strictness choice; if the mesh prompt turns out to emit string-only FAQ lists, this silently deletes the section and only `dropped` will show it.

   **RULING:** accepted — an answer cannot be invented, and `dropped` records the deletion. If the mesh prompt turns out to emit string-only FAQ lists, fix the prompt; do not loosen the normalizer into rendering half a Q&A.

#### Task 4 — `draft.ts`

1. **`normalizeDraft` takes `maxPages: number` with no bound in the type.** I clamp `< 1` up to `1` rather than erroring, since returning `VALIDATION_ERROR` for a caller bug would surface to the end user as a generation failure. A `maxPages` of `0` from a caller is a bug worth surfacing some other way.

   **RULING:** correct — a caller bug must not surface to the end user as a generation failure. Clamping `maxPages < 1` up to 1 is the right call; surface the caller bug through logging, not through a failed render the user paid credits for.

#### Task 5 — `slug.ts`

1. **`resolveSlug`'s default `randomSuffix` uses `Math.random`.** Against a *globally* unique index this is a collision-probability argument, not a correctness one — `isTaken` still guards every candidate — but `crypto.randomUUID()`/`getRandomValues` would be the stronger default. The contract fixes `ResolveSlugDeps { randomSuffix?: () => string }` with no way to pass an RNG source, so switching the default later is a non-breaking one-line change inside `slug.ts`.

   **RULING:** accepted — `isTaken` guards every candidate, so this is a collision-probability argument, not a correctness one; switching the default to `crypto.getRandomValues` later is a one-line, non-breaking change inside `slug.ts`.

2. **The 14-probe bound is 14 sequential database round-trips in the worst case.** Acceptable for a once-per-site operation, but if `isTaken` is ever backed by a slow path this becomes a visible stall inside the `withCredits` window for a 100-credit task. Worth a note in the wt-web mount docs.

   **RULING:** accepted — a once-per-site operation. Note it in the wt-web mount docs so a slow `isSlugTaken` is recognised as a stall inside the `withCredits` window rather than a hang.

3. **`slugify`'s 48-char cap is not enforced on the final resolved slug.** `resolveSlug` appends `-9` or `-abc123` *after* the cap, so the maximum emitted slug is 55 characters. That is still inside the 63-char DNS label limit, and `sites.slug` is untyped `text`, so nothing breaks — but the cap constant name reads as a guarantee it does not actually make about the return value.

   **RULING:** accepted — 55 characters is inside the 63-char DNS label limit and `sites.slug` is untyped `text`, so nothing breaks. Rename the constant if it ever starts reading as a guarantee about the return value.

#### Task 6 — `to-rows.ts`

1. **`SiteRowsPage.page` typed via `Omit<SitePageInsert, …>` inherits the schema's post-default optionality.** `SitePageInsertSchema` declares `path: z.string().default('/')` and `sort: z.int().default(0)`, so `z.infer` makes both **required** on the output type — which is what we want — but a future `SiteInsertSchema` edit that adds a `.optional()` field would silently loosen `SiteRows` with no test failure anywhere in `packages/sites`. The Task 6 conformance tests catch fields we emit *wrongly*, not fields the schema starts *requiring*. A `z.strictObject`-style exactness test would close this; I did not add one because it would fail the moment wt-db adds a legitimately optional column. Flagging for the reviewer.

   **RULING:** accepted for v0 — an exactness test would fail the moment wt-db adds a legitimately optional column. The dangerous direction is a schema that starts *requiring* a field, and that surfaces at the call site under `turbo typecheck`.

2. **Child rows cannot be parsed by their own frozen schema as emitted.** Because `site_id`/`page_id`/`workspace_id` are omitted, `SitePageInsertSchema.safeParse(rows.pages[0].page)` is *expected* to fail. The conformance tests therefore stamp fake ids first, which means the real contract being tested is "everything except the ids conforms". That is the strongest available assertion given the store-owns-ids decision, but it is weaker than the design doc's §10 line *"conformance to `SitePageInsertSchema`"* implies. I followed the interface contract as written and added an explicit test (`emits a page row the frozen schema REJECTS without site_id`) so the gap is documented in the suite rather than hidden.

   **RULING:** accepted, and IMPORTANT: record explicitly that the conformance test proves "everything except the store-owned ids conforms", which is weaker than the design doc §10 wording implies. Keep the `emits a page row the frozen schema REJECTS without site_id` test so the gap stays visible in the suite rather than hidden.

#### Task 7 — `oklch.ts`

1. **`parseOklch` rejects three forms that are legal CSS**: leading-dot decimals (`.62`), uppercase `OKLCH(...)`, and comma separation. All three are valid per CSS Color Level 4. This is intentional strictness — we are the only writer of these values, so anything outside our own output grammar is by definition foreign input — but it means a hand-edited `workspace_themes` row using legal CSS will silently drop the theme rather than error. Worth a note in `packages/sites/CLAUDE.md`.

   **RULING:** correct — we are the only writer of these values, so anything outside our own output grammar is foreign input by definition. Note it in `packages/sites/CLAUDE.md` (Task 18) so a hand-edited `workspace_themes` row is diagnosable rather than mysterious.

2. **`parseOklch` accepts a negative lightness** (the contract's grammar implies a numeric parse, not a range check). Nothing downstream breaks — the guard clamps and the sRGB encoder clamps — but it means `oklch(-5 0.14 250)` is "parseable" and silently renders as black rather than being rejected as unusable. If the contract intends `l ∈ [0, 1]`, say so and I will tighten the parse.

   **RULING:** accepted — the guard clamps and the sRGB encoder clamps, so a negative lightness renders as black rather than escaping anywhere. Tightening the parse to `l ∈ [0, 1]` is a post-Alpha change, not a v0 blocker.

#### Task 8 — `readability.ts`

1. **The Task 8 brief's stated fallback trigger does not exist.** The brief asks for "a pathological input (zero chroma mid-grey) hitting the fallback". I swept the whole input space and it does not: `oklch(0.55 0 0)` reads 4.88:1 against white at step 0 and returns immediately, and *every* finite `(l, c, h)` converges before the 32-step budget expires, because lightness clamps at 0 and pure black is 21:1. The fallback branch is reachable **only** via a non-finite component (`NaN`/`Infinity`), which makes every `>=` comparison false forever. I wrote the fallback test against that real trigger and documented why in the test name. If the fallback is meant to be unreachable for finite input, that is worth stating explicitly in the contract.

   **RULING:** correct, and verified — sweeping the input space shows only a non-finite component reaches the fallback. Keep the test named against that real trigger, and state the unreachability-for-finite-input explicitly in the contract.

2. **The fallback drops chroma; the apps/web original keeps it.** `brand-theme.ts:48` returns `formatOklch(0, c, h)`. Since the only path into the fallback carries a non-finite component, `c` may itself be `NaN` — which would emit `oklch(0 NaN 20)` into a customer's stylesheet. I return `{ l: 0, c: 0, h: finite(h) ? h : 0 }` instead, which is provably 21:1 and is what makes the Task 8 property test hold unconditionally. This is the one place I did not preserve the ported code exactly, and it is deliberate.

   **RULING:** the deviation from the apps/web original is CORRECT and deliberate — keep it. `formatOklch(0, c, h)` could emit `oklch(0 NaN 20)` into a customer's stylesheet; `{ l: 0, c: 0, h: finite(h) ? h : 0 }` is provably 21:1 and is what makes the property test hold unconditionally.

3. **`INK_RGB` is not exported, so it is pinned only indirectly.** The contract text names the constant but the export list for `readability.ts` has three entries and `INK_RGB` is not among them. I kept it module-private and pinned it in the test by recomputing `#131313` locally and asserting `guarded.ratio === contrastRatio(primary, localInk)`. If `tokens.css` ever changes `--ink`, that test fails — but only because the local literal and the module literal disagree, which is the intended signal. Exporting it would be a cleaner pin.

   **RULING:** accepted — the test recomputes `#131313` locally, so a change to `--ink` in `tokens.css` fails the suite; that is the intended signal, even though exporting the constant would be a cleaner pin.

#### Task 9 — `theme/css.ts`

1. **`--acc` gets no readability guard, unlike apps/web.** `brand-theme.ts:77` runs the accent through `darkenForTextOnWhite` so it reads as text on a light surface. The locked contract for `readability.ts` exports exactly three symbols and none is that function, and the Task 9 derivation list does not mention `--acc`, so I pass the accent through reformatted only. A workspace whose accent is a pale yellow will therefore ship accent text at roughly 1.2:1 on a white section background. Recommend either adding `darkenForTextOnWhite` to the `readability.ts` contract or accepting this as a known v0 gap.

   **RULING:** accepted as a known v0 gap — `readability.ts`'s contract exports exactly three symbols and none is `darkenForTextOnWhite`. Revisit post-Alpha alongside the Brand Skin work, and record it in `packages/sites/CLAUDE.md`.

#### Task 10 — contracts (`render/context.ts`, `deploy/port.ts`)

1. **The contract's task numbering is not a valid build order.** `document.ts` (Task 10) must import the six renderers (Task 11), `contact.ts` (Task 11) must import `renderLeadForm` (Task 12), and `bundle.ts` (Task 12) must import `SiteBundle`/`BundleFile` from `deploy/port.ts` (Task 13). I followed the contract's file placement exactly and added explicit order notes rather than stubbing anything. Real execution order: **12A → 11 → 10 → 13(port types) → 12B**. Suggest renumbering in the final plan.

   **RULING:** resolved — this plan renumbers the tasks into a valid build order, so the numbers quoted in the concern are the pre-renumbering ones. Contracts land in Task 10, `form.ts` in Task 11, the renderers in Task 12, `document.ts` in Task 13 and `bundle.ts` in Task 14; nothing is red between tasks and no "build 12A first" footnote survives.

2. **`RenderContext` living in `document.ts` creates a type-only import cycle.** Every `sections/*.ts` file imports `type RenderContext` from `../document` while `document.ts` imports those files' values. TypeScript erases the type import so there is no runtime cycle, but it is fragile — one accidental value import from `../document` inside a section file turns it into a real cycle. `RenderContext` would be safer in its own `src/render/context.ts`.

   **RULING:** resolved by moving `RenderContext` into `src/render/context.ts` (this task). Every `sections/*.ts` file imports it from a leaf module, so the type-only cycle is eliminated rather than documented — one accidental value import can no longer turn it into a real one.

3. **`SiteBundle` / `BundleFile` live in Task 13 but Task 12 produces them.** The locked contract places both interfaces under `src/deploy/port.ts` (Task 13) while `renderBundle` (Task 12) returns `SiteBundle`. Executed in numeric order, Task 12 cannot typecheck until Task 13 exists. Either move Tasks 13's `port.ts` type-only block earlier, or accept that `pnpm --filter @sahoda/sites typecheck` is red between Task 12 and Task 13. I followed the contract and did not relocate the types.

   **RULING:** resolved by the same renumbering — `SiteBundle`/`BundleFile` land here in Task 10, and `renderBundle` (Task 14) imports them from a module that already exists. The numbers quoted in the concern are the pre-renumbering ones.

4. **`appendHistory` and `DEPLOY_HISTORY_LIMIT` are additions to `port.ts` not named in the contract.** The contract specifies `history: SiteDeployHistoryEntry[] // max 5` but no mechanism to enforce it. The schema's `.max(5)` only *rejects*; it does not *evict*, so a state built with 6 entries would fail to persist rather than dropping the oldest. Both deployers need identical capping logic, so I extracted it rather than duplicating. If the contract is meant to be exhaustive as a module surface, these two should be added to it explicitly, and Task 18's `index.ts` should decide whether they are public or internal — I would keep them internal.

   **RULING:** correct addition, keep — and keep it internal. The schema's `.max(5)` rejects rather than evicts, and both deployers need identical capping logic, so `appendHistory` and `DEPLOY_HISTORY_LIMIT` belong in `port.ts` and are NOT re-exported from `src/index.ts`.

#### Task 11 — `form.ts`

1. **`renderContact` takes `submitLabel` from model output and the default lives in `form.ts`.** `contact.ts` passes `''` when the model omitted a label, and `form.ts` substitutes `'Send enquiry'`. This keeps the default in one place, but it means `renderLeadForm('')` is a meaningful call — worth a comment at the call site if that reads as accidental.

   **RULING:** accepted — one default, in one place. `contact.ts` carries a comment at the call site so `renderLeadForm('')` does not read as accidental, and Task 11's task text states the rule explicitly.

#### Task 12 — the six section renderers

1. **`_ctx` is unused in five of the six renderers.** If `noUnusedParameters` is on in the copied `tsconfig.json`, confirm the underscore prefix is honored; otherwise these need `// eslint-disable` or the parameter must be consumed. The contract fixes the two-parameter signature, so the parameter cannot simply be dropped.

   **RULING:** accepted — confirm during Task 1 that the copied `tsconfig.json` honours the underscore prefix for `noUnusedParameters`. The two-parameter signature is fixed by the contract and must not be dropped; if the prefix is not honoured, consume the parameter rather than disabling the rule.

#### Task 13 — `document.ts`

1. **The Outfit webfont is loaded from `fonts.googleapis.com`.** docs/07 mandates Outfit and the package cannot self-host, so generated tenant sites make a third-party request. No script is involved, so the no-inline-JS invariant holds, but it is a privacy/GDPR consideration for customer-facing pages and should be an explicit product decision, not a renderer default.

   **RULING:** accepted for v0 and escalated — docs/07 mandates Outfit and this package cannot self-host, so a generated tenant page makes a third-party font request. No script is involved, so the no-inline-JS invariant holds. It is a privacy decision, not a renderer default: if it is not ruled on before Alpha, file it alongside the REQUESTS.md items.

#### Task 14 — `bundle.ts`

1. **`renderStylesheet(ctx)` vs. "inline tokens.css" pull in opposite directions.** Task 13 requires tokens.css inline in `<head>`; Task 14 requires the bundle to emit a stylesheet file. I resolved it by splitting responsibilities — `<head>` inlines tokens + theme custom properties (needed before first paint), `styles.css` carries layout — so nothing is duplicated. If the intent was one self-contained file per page with no external CSS request, `styles.css` becomes dead weight and should be dropped from the bundle.

   **RULING:** accepted — `<head>` inlines tokens plus the theme custom properties because they must resolve before first paint, and `styles.css` carries layout only, so nothing is duplicated. The split stands for v0; dropping `styles.css` would mean re-inlining layout into every page.

#### Task 16 — transport + Cloudflare deployer

1. **`DeployContext.baseDomain` and `CloudflareConfig.baseDomain` are redundant.** Both are in the contract, and nothing specifies precedence. I implemented `ctx.baseDomain ?? config.baseDomain` (per-deploy override wins, config is the default) and pinned both branches with tests. If the intent was the opposite, one test flips. A stronger design would drop one of the two fields, since two sources of the domain is exactly the kind of ambiguity that produces a wrong-but-plausible URL — the failure mode this feature exists to prevent.

   **RULING:** accepted — `ctx.baseDomain ?? config.baseDomain` (the per-deploy override wins, config is the default), with both branches pinned by test. Collapsing the two fields into one is a post-Alpha contract change, not a v0 edit.

2. **`TransportRequest.body?: string` drops `Uint8Array`.** `packages/publishing`'s version allows bytes (X media upload needs it). Sites bundles are text, so `string` is sufficient today, but if a later task needs to upload a binary asset (a favicon, an OG image) this port must widen — and widening it is a change to a locked signature. Flagging now rather than discovering it mid-implementation.

   **RULING:** accepted — `string` is sufficient while every bundle file is text. Widening to `Uint8Array` is a deliberate contract change to be made when a binary asset actually ships, not speculatively.

3. **Fixture bodies are JSON-encoded strings, unlike `packages/publishing`'s fixtures.** This follows from `TransportResponse.body: string` combined with `fixtureTransport(recorded: TransportResponse)` — the publishing version took a separate `RecordedResponse` with `body?: unknown` and stringified it. The upside is a fixture that is byte-identical to the wire (and lets the malformed-JSON case be recorded at all, which the publishing shape cannot express); the downside is less readable fixture files. I judged the malformed-body coverage worth it, but it is a deliberate divergence from the lane's existing fixtures.

   **RULING:** accepted — a fixture that is byte-identical to the wire is worth the readability cost, and it is the only shape that can record the malformed-JSON case at all. The divergence from `packages/publishing`'s fixtures is intentional and should be noted where both lanes are read together.

4. **The Cloudflare worker embeds assets in the script body.** Workers-for-Platforms also supports a static-assets binding, which would be the better production choice for a site of any size (script-size limits apply to the embedded approach). The embedded map is chosen because it is one PUT with no second upload flow, matching the design doc's 90-minute time-box on Cloudflare tooling. This should be revisited before a real site with images ships; it is a correctness cliff, not a style preference.

   **RULING:** accepted for v0 and flagged — the embedded asset map is one PUT and fits the design doc's 90-minute Cloudflare time-box. Revisit before a real site with images ships: script-size limits make this a correctness cliff, not a style preference.

#### Task 17 — `store.ts`

1. **`src/store.ts` is type-only, so the Task 17 test cannot fail at runtime for the right reason.** `import type { SiteStore }` is erased by esbuild, so `vitest` alone would report green against a missing `store.ts` — the test would be exercising only `resolveSlug` and `toRows`. I made Step 2 run `typecheck` first so the red step is genuine (`TS2307`). If the contract could grow one runtime value in `store.ts` — e.g. `export const SITE_STORE_METHODS = ['isSlugTaken','createSite','recordDeploy'] as const` — the test would fail at runtime for the honest reason and could also assert the verb set is exactly three. I did not add it, since the contract is locked.

   **RULING:** keep the `typecheck`-first red step, AND add `export const SITE_STORE_METHODS = ['isSlugTaken', 'createSite', 'recordDeploy'] as const` to `store.ts` so the test fails at runtime for the honest reason and can assert the verb set is exactly three. Task 17 is updated accordingly; the constant stays out of `src/index.ts`.

2. **The contract defines no exported composition helper, so the "composed call site" lives in the test file.** `persistSite` / `persistDeploy` in `store.test.ts` are a reference implementation of glue that wt-web will write independently. That means the tested behaviour — a throwing store becoming a typed `Result` — is pinned only against a copy of the glue, not against the code that ships. wt-web can drift from it and no test in this package notices. The doc comment is the only enforcement. If a `createSiteFromDraft(draft, store, ctx): Promise<Result<{siteId, slug}>>` were added to the contract, this would become a real guarantee instead of a documented hope.

   **RULING:** accepted for v0 and called out in REQUESTS.md as a known seam — the doc comment on `SiteStore` is the enforcement. If `createSiteFromDraft(draft, store, ctx)` is ever added to the contract, this becomes a real guarantee instead of a documented hope.

3. **`toRows` output shape is asserted only structurally in Task 17.** `SiteRowsPage.sections` is `Omit<SiteSectionInsert, 'page_id' | 'workspace_id'>`, so `content` is `JsonbSchema` and the contract does not state how `NormalizedSection.section.content` maps into it. I assert `kind`, `sort`, and array lengths, and avoid asserting `content.headline`, since that mapping belongs to Task 6's tests. If Task 6 chose to store `raw` rather than the normalized content, nothing in Task 17 would catch it.

   **RULING:** accepted — the `content` mapping belongs to Task 6's tests, where it is asserted byte-for-byte and through a JSON round-trip. Task 17 asserts only what the port itself is responsible for.

4. **`SiteDeployState` has no workspace binding, and `recordDeploy(siteId, state)` takes no `workspaceId`.** A service-role implementation updating `sites` by `id` alone is one missing `.eq('workspace_id', …)` away from writing deploy state onto another tenant's site. `sites` has `unique (id, workspace_id)` specifically to make that filter cheap. The port's signature does not let the caller pass the tenant, so the implementation must re-read it. Documented in the hard requirements; a `workspaceId` parameter would have made it structural.

   **RULING:** accepted for v0 — hard requirement 4 in the `SiteStore` doc comment now states the `.eq('workspace_id', …)` obligation explicitly. Adding `workspaceId` to `recordDeploy` would make it structural and should be the first change if the port reopens.

#### Task 18 — public surface

1. **The `EXPECTED_VALUE_EXPORTS` list in Task 18 is a judgement call the contract does not settle.** The contract fixes the sentinel, the "no `export *`" rule, and the header comment, but not which of the 60-odd symbols across Tasks 1–17 are public. I drew the line at "renderBundle is the only way to produce HTML" to keep the escaping gate unbypassable. If a later task needs `renderDocument` directly, the exact-surface test will fail — deliberately, so the decision gets made explicitly rather than by accretion.

   **RULING:** accepted — "`renderBundle` is the only way to produce HTML" is the right line, because it is what keeps the escaping gate unbypassable. The exact-surface test makes any later widening an explicit decision rather than accretion.
